In November 2022, Therapy Brands — a private-equity-backed health IT company focused on mental, behavioral, and rehabilitative therapy — acquired The Echo Group. Therapy Brands subsequently rebranded to **Ensora Health** in early 2025, unifying its portfolio of therapy-focused software products under a single brand.\n     9→\n    10→Ensora Health's broader portfolio includes TheraNest (mental health practice management), Fusion (PT/OT/speech therapy practice management), and EchoVantage. The Ensora parent describes serving \"over 30,000 therapy practices\" across its entire portfolio, though it's unclear how many of those specifically use EchoVantage versus other Ensora products.\n    11→\n    12→EchoVantage is specifically positioned for **community behavioral health agencies** — a distinct market from solo/small-group private practices (which TheraNest targets). EchoVantage serves organizations ranging from small community behavioral health agencies to comprehensive enterprise-wide organizations, including mental health clinics, substance use recovery (SUR) centers, intellectual and developmental disabilities (I/DD) services, drug and alcohol centers, crisis intervention departments, foster care, residential care, correctional health facilities, and county-level behavioral health programs.\n    13→\n    14→## Product: EchoVantage\n    15→\n    16→CHPL ID: 11129\n    17→\n    18→### What It Is\n    19→\n    20→EchoVantage is a **web-based, integrated EHR, billing, and practice management platform** purpose-built for behavioral healthcare. Its signature feature is the **Visual Health Record (VHR)** — a graphical timeline interface that presents clinical information (progress notes, medications, assessments, treatment plans) along a visual continuum of care, showing events in relationship to each other to support clinical understanding.\n    21→\n    22→The certified product appears to be the complete EchoVantage platform, not a module of something larger. It is available as both cloud-based and on-premise deployment. Within Ensora Health's portfolio, EchoVantage is the product aimed at larger community behavioral health organizations, whereas TheraNest serves smaller private practices and Fusion serves rehab therapy — these are distinct products, not modules of EchoVantage.\n    23→\n    24→The certified criteria cover clinical documentation (a)(3), (a)(5), (a)(12), (a)(14), (a)(15); transitions of care (b)(1); FHIR APIs (g)(7), (g)(10); and direct messaging (h)(1). Notably absent from certification are CPOE criteria (a)(1)-(a)(2), drug interaction checking (a)(4), lab results (a)(9), patient portal/VDT (e)(1), and public health reporting (f) criteria — consistent with EchoVantage being a behavioral health system rather than a general ambulatory EHR.\n    25→\n    26→### Users & Market\n    27→\n    28→**Target organizations:**\n    29→- Community mental health centers/agencies (CMHCs)\n    30→- Substance use recovery / drug and alcohol treatment centers\n    31→- Intellectual and developmental disabilities (I/DD) service providers\n    32→- Crisis intervention and prevention departments\n    33→- Foster care and residential care programs\n    34→- Correctional health facilities\n    35→- County behavioral health programs\n    36→\n    37→**Day-to-day users include:**\n    38→- **Clinicians/therapists** — for clinical documentation, treatment planning, assessments, progress notes\n    39→- **Prescribers** — for e-prescribing (via DrFirst integration)\n    40→- **Billing staff** — for claims submission, eligibility verification, payment processing\n    41→- **Administrators** — for scheduling, workflow configuration, compliance\n    42→- **Executives/managers** — for reporting dashboards, productivity metrics, financial oversight\n    43→- **Supervisors** — for reviewing staff activity, caseloads, and performance\n    44→\n    45→**Market position:** EchoVantage occupies a niche in the community behavioral health market — larger and more complex organizations than solo/small-group practices (which use products like TheraNest) but generally not hospital systems. The Echo Group has been in this space since the early 1980s, giving it deep domain expertise. Specific customer counts for EchoVantage are not publicly disclosed. Third-party review sites show limited review volume (roughly 10-15 reviews on sites like Capterra and Crozdesk), suggesting a relatively small but specialized user base. The product has a user satisfaction rating of approximately 86% based on limited reviews.\n    46→\n    47→No specific notable customer case studies were found publicly available.\n    48→\n    49→### In November 2022, Therapy Brands — a private-equity-backed health IT company focused on mental, behavioral, and rehabilitative therapy — acquired The Echo Group. Therapy Brands subsequently rebranded to **Ensora Health** in early 2025, unifying its portfolio of therapy-focused software products under a single brand.\n\nEnsora Health's broader portfolio includes TheraNest (mental health practice management), Fusion (PT/OT/speech therapy practice management), and EchoVantage. The Ensora parent describes serving \"over 30,000 therapy practices\" across its entire portfolio, though it's unclear how many of those specifically use EchoVantage versus other Ensora products.\n\nEchoVantage is specifically positioned for **community behavioral health agencies** — a distinct market from solo/small-group private practices (which TheraNest targets). EchoVantage serves organizations ranging from small community behavioral health agencies to comprehensive enterprise-wide organizations, including mental health clinics, substance use recovery (SUR) centers, intellectual and developmental disabilities (I/DD) services, drug and alcohol centers, crisis intervention departments, foster care, residential care, correctional health facilities, and county-level behavioral health programs.\n\n## Product: EchoVantage\n\nCHPL ID: 11129\n\n### What It Is\n\nEchoVantage is a **web-based, integrated EHR, billing, and practice management platform** purpose-built for behavioral healthcare. Its signature feature is the **Visual Health Record (VHR)** — a graphical timeline interface that presents clinical information (progress notes, medications, assessments, treatment plans) along a visual continuum of care, showing events in relationship to each other to support clinical understanding.\n\nThe certified product appears to be the complete EchoVantage platform, not a module of something larger. It is available as both cloud-based and on-premise deployment. Within Ensora Health's portfolio, EchoVantage is the product aimed at larger community behavioral health organizations, whereas TheraNest serves smaller private practices and Fusion serves rehab therapy — these are distinct products, not modules of EchoVantage.\n\nThe certified criteria cover clinical documentation (a)(3), (a)(5), (a)(12), (a)(14), (a)(15); transitions of care (b)(1); FHIR APIs (g)(7), (g)(10); and direct messaging (h)(1). Notably absent from certification are CPOE criteria (a)(1)-(a)(2), drug interaction checking (a)(4), lab results (a)(9), patient portal/VDT (e)(1), and public health reporting (f) criteria — consistent with EchoVantage being a behavioral health system rather than a general ambulatory EHR.\n\n### Users & Market\n\n**Target organizations:**\n- Community mental health centers/agencies (CMHCs)\n- Substance use recovery / drug and alcohol treatment centers\n- Intellectual and developmental disabilities (I/DD) service providers\n- Crisis intervention and prevention departments\n- Foster care and residential care programs\n- Correctional health facilities\n- County behavioral health programs\n\n**Day-to-day users include:**\n- **Clinicians/therapists** — for clinical documentation, treatment planning, assessments, progress notes\n- **Prescribers** — for e-prescribing (via DrFirst integration)\n- **Billing staff** — for claims submission, eligibility verification, payment processing\n- **Administrators** — for scheduling, workflow configuration, compliance\n- **Executives/managers** — for reporting dashboards, productivity metrics, financial oversight\n- **Supervisors** — for reviewing staff activity, caseloads, and performance\n\n**Market position:** EchoVantage occupies a niche in the community behavioral health market — larger and more complex organizations than solo/small-group practices (which use products like TheraNest) but generally not hospital systems. The Echo Group has been in this space since the early 1980s, giving it deep domain expertise. Specific customer counts for EchoVantage are not publicly disclosed. Third-party review sites show limited review volume (roughly 10-15 reviews on sites like Capterra and Crozdesk), suggesting a relatively small but specialized user base. The product has a user satisfaction rating of approximately 86% based on limited reviews.\n\nNo specific notable customer case studies were found publicly available.\n\n### Modules & Functionality\n\nBased on vendor materials, press releases, and third-party review sites, EchoVantage includes the following functional areas:\n\n**Clinical / EHR:**\n- **Visual Health Record (VHR):** engagement feature)\n- Medication alerts\n\n**Telehealth:**\n- HIPAA-compliant video telehealth\n- Supports individual and group sessions\n\n**Reporting & Analytics:**\n- Customizable reporting dashboards (30+ presets available, plus custom designs)\n- Practice health metrics\n- Client outcomes tracking\n- Real-time productivity and financial data\n- Staff activity, caseload, and performance visibility for supervisors\n\n**State Reporting:**\n- State-specific reporting capability for community mental health and substance abuse compliance requirements\n- Configured per state to address specific mandatory reporting formats\n\n**Medication Assisted Treatment (MAT) Module** (announced for H1 2025):\n- Liquid Methadone dosing, dispensing, and tracking\n- Real-time coordination between front desk, medical, counseling, and billing staff\n- Designed for substance use recovery organizations\n\n**Document Management:**\n- Document storage and management integrated with the clinical intake information** — health status, medical history, conditions captured during intake\n- **Clinical documentation** — progress notes, therapy session notes, clinical narratives\n- **Treatment and on-going annual fees to support the same. 170.315 (a)(3), 170.315 (a)(5), 170.315 (a)(12), 170.315 (a)(14), 170.315 (a)(15), 170.315 (b)(1), 170.315 (b)(10), 170.315 (b)(11), 170.315 (d)(1), (d)(13)</strong></p>\\n\\n\\n\\n<p>EchoVantage offers organizations, as an add on service, the ability to have Single Sign On. By using Single Sign On providers can configure their log in settings to include multi-factor authentication (MFA).</p>\\n</div>\\n\\n\\n\\n<div class=\\\"wp-block-group add-divider padding-top30 padding-bottom30 is-vertical is-layout-flex wp-container-core-group-is-layout-8cf370e7 wp-block-group-is-layout-flex\\\">\\n<h4 class=\\\"wp-block-heading has-primary-black-color has-text-color has-link-color wp-elements-f94996083e230a5e319175c8dcf1f060\\\" id=\\\"h-electronic-health-information-ehi-export\\\">Electronic Health Information (EHI) Export</h4>\\n\\n\\n\\n<p><strong>Single Patient Export</strong><br>EchoVantage EHR allows a user to export electronic health information (EHI) for a single patient without developer assistance using the following standardized file formats explained below</p>\\n\\n\\n\\n<p><strong>Patient Population Export<br></strong>EchoVantage EHR can export all the data for a patient population using the following standardized formats explained below. This export can be requested by submitting a support ticket via Salesforce.</p>\\n\\n\\n\\n<p>The content included in the export might vary based on a variety of factors, including:</p>\\n\\n\\n\\n<ul class=\\\"wp-block-list\\\">\\n<li>The software application(s) in use at the health system that generated the files.</li>\\n\\n\\n\\n<li>The software version in use at the health system that generated the files.</li>\\n\\n\\n\\n<li>The documentation and software use practices in place at the health system that generated the files.</li>\\n\\n\\n\\n<li>Configuration decisions made by the health system that generated the files.</li>\\n\\n\\n\\n<li>The health system including materials not sourced from the application</li>\\n</ul>\\n\\n\\n\\n<p><br><a href=\\\"https://ensorahealth.com/wp-content/uploads/2025/08/Electronic-Health-Information-Export_EchoVantage.pdf\\\">File Formats</a></p>\\n</div>\\n\\n\\n\\n<div class=\\\"wp-block-group add-divider wp-block-group-is-layout-flex\\\">\\n<h3 class=\\\"wp-block-heading has-ocean-blue-color has-text-color has-link-color wp-elements-c8299bf382d2b5974322be3a830d7cd4\\\" id=\\\"h-onc-certification-criteria-for-health-it-0\\\">ONC Certification Criteria for Health IT</h3>\\n\\n\\n\\n<p>This Health IT Module is compliant with the ONC Certification Criteria for Health IT and has<br>been certified by an ONC-ACB in accordance with the applicable certification criteria adopted by<br>the Secretary of Health and Human Services. This certification does not represent an<br>endorsement by the U.S. Department of Health and Human Services.</p>\\n\\n\\n\\n<p><strong>Developer Name:</strong>\\u00a0The Echo Group<br><strong>Product Name:</strong>\\u00a0EchoVantage<br><strong>Product Version:</strong>\\u00a03<br><strong>Address:</strong>\\u00a03504 7th Ave S Ste 83951, Birmingham AL 35222-3211<br><strong>Email:</strong>\\u00a0kathy.bunker@ensorahealth.com<br><strong>Contact Name:</strong>\\u00a0Kathy Bunker<br><strong>Certification Number:</strong>\\u00a015.04.04.2425.Echo.13.02.1.221227<br><strong>Certified Date:</strong>\\u00a012/27/2022<br><strong>Additional Software:</strong>\\u00a0EMR Direct Interoperability Engine 2017, Microsoft Azure AD SSO, phiMail Server</p>\\n\\n\\n\\n<figure class=\\\"wp-block-image size-large\\\"><img loading=\\\"lazy\\\" decoding=\\\"async\\\" width=\\\"461\\\" height=\\\"600\\\" src=\\\"https://ensorahealth.com/wp-content/uploads/2025/05/EchoVantage-Certificate-461x600.jpg\\\" alt=\\\"\\\" class=\\\"wp-image-6720\\\"/></figure>\\n</div>\\n\\n\\n\\n<div class=\\\"wp-block-group add-divider padding-top30 padding-bottom30 is-vertical is-layout-flex wp-container-core-group-is-layout-8cf370e7 wp-block-group-is-layout-flex\\\">\\n<h2 class=\\\"wp-block-heading has-ocean-blue-color has-text-color has-link-color wp-elements-855cea7715b509c75df67a8e089c915b\\\" id=\\\"h-attestation-disclosure\\\">Attestation Disclosure</h2>\\n\\n\\n\\n<p>This certified product-version requires an annual contractual commitment that may include one-time costs to establish interfaces for reporting, lab services, secure email and on-going annual fees to support the same. 170.315 (a)(3), 170.315 (a)(5), 170.315 (a)(12), 170.315 (a)(14), 170.315 (a)(15), 170.315 (b)(1), 170.315 (b)(10), 170.315 (b)(11), 170.315 (d)(1), 170.315 (d)(2), 170.315 (d)(3), 170.315 (d)(4), 170.315 (d)(5), 170.315 (d)(6), 170.315 (d)(7), 170.315 (d)(8), 170.315 (d)(9), 170.315 (d)(12), 170.315 (d)(13), 170.315 (g)(3), 170.315 (g)(4), 170.315 (g)(5), 170.315 (g)(6), 170.315 (g)(7), 170.315 (g)(9), 170.315 (g)(10), 170.315 (h)(1)</p>\\n\\n\\n\\n<p><strong>Multi-Factor Authentication 170.315 (d)(13)</strong></p>\\n\\n\\n\\n<p>EchoVantage offers organizations, as an add on service, the ability to have Single Sign On. By using Single Sign On providers can configure their log in settings to include multi-factor authentication (MFA).</p>\\n</div>\\n\\n\\n\\n<div class=\\\"wp-block-group add-divider padding-top30 padding-bottom30 is-vertical is-layout-flex wp-container-core-group-is-layout-8cf370e7 wp-block-group-is-layout-flex\\\">\\n<h4 class=\\\"wp-block-heading has-primary-black-color has-text-color has-link-color wp-elements-f94996083e230a5e319175c8dcf1f060\\\" id=\\\"h-electronic-health-information-ehi-export\\\">Electronic Health Information (EHI) Export</h4>\\n\\n\\n\\n<p><strong>Single Patient Export</strong><br>EchoVantage EHR allows a user to export electronic health information (EHI) for a single patient without developer assistance using the following standardized file formats explained below</p>\\n\\n\\n\\n<p><strong>Patient Population Export<br></strong>EchoVantage EHR can export all the data for a patient population using the following standardized formats explained below. This export can be requested by submitting a support ticket via Salesforce.</p>\\n\\n\\n\\n<p>The content included in the export might vary based on a variety of factors, including:</p>\\n\\n\\n\\n<ul class=\\\"wp-block-list\\\">\\n<li>The software application(s) in use at the health system that generated the files.</li>\\n\\n\\n\\n<li>The software version in use at the health system that generated the files.</li>\\n\\n\\n\\n<li>The documentation and software use practices in place at the health system that generated the files.</li>\\n\\n\\n\\n<li>Configuration decisions made by the health system that generated the files.</li>\\n\\n\\n\\n<li>The health system including materials not sourced from the application</li>\\n</ul>\\n\\n\\n\\n<p><br><a href=\\\"https://ensorahealth.com/wp-content/uploads/2025/08/Electronic-Health-Information-Export_EchoVantage.pdf\\\">File Formats</a></p>\\n</div>\\n\\n\\n\\n<div class=\\\"wp-block-group add-divider padding-top30 padding-bottom30 is-vertical is-layout-flex wp-container-core-group-is-layout-8cf370e7 wp-block-group-is-layout-flex\\\">\\n<h4 class=\\\"wp-block-heading has-primary-black-color has-text-color has-link-color wp-elements-6a50e3a534113f2e3291b7c72bc452b5\\\" id=\\\"h-echovantage-fhir-api\\\">EchoVantage FHIR API</h4>\\n\\n\\n\\n<p>Our FHIR API is in compliance with Health IT certification criteria adopted in \\u00a7\\u2009170.315(g)(7) through (10).</p>\\n\\n\\n\\n<p><a href=\\\"https://ensorahealth.com/onc/echo/echo-api/\\\">EchoVantage FHIR API Documentation</a></p>\\n\\n\\n\\n<p><a href=\\\"https://ensorahealth.com/onc/echo/fhir/r4/Endpoint\\\">EchoVantage FHIR Endpoint</a></p>\\n</div>\\n\\n\\n\\n<div class=\\\"wp-block-group add-divider padding-top30 padding-bottom10 is-vertical is-layout-flex wp-container-core-group-is-layout-8cf370e7 wp-block-group-is-layout-flex\\\">\\n<h2 class=\\\"wp-block-heading has-ocean-blue-color {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-6","id":"msg_01GW2co4o63YZTRE1BuyiFsf","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01Bs9TAz9hhA5SU7YtWf6gtj","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/home/jmandel/hobby/ehi-export-analysis/results/the-echo-group--echovantage/ehi-export-report.md","content":"# The Echo Group (Ensora Health) — EHI Export Documentation\n\nCollected: 2026-02-16\n\n## Source\n- Registered URL: https://ensorahealth.com/onc/echo/\n- CHPL IDs: 11129\n- Product: EchoVantage, Version 3\n- Developer: The Echo Group (now part of Ensora Health)\n- Certification Date: 2022-12-27\n\n## Navigation Journal\n\n### Step 1: Probe the registered URL\n```bash\ncurl -sI -L \"https://ensorahealth.com/onc/echo/\" -H 'User-Agent: Mozilla/5.0'\n```\nResult: HTTP 200, Content-Type: text/html; charset=UTF-8. WordPress site on Cloudflare. No redirects.\n\n### Step 2: Fetch and examine the page\n```bash\ncurl -sL \"https://ensorahealth.com/onc/echo/\" -H 'User-Agent: Mozilla/5.0' -o /tmp/echo-page.html\n```\nThe page is a standard ONC certification compliance page for EchoVantage by Ensora Health. It contains:\n- ONC certification details (developer, product, version, certification number)\n- Attestation Disclosure listing all certified criteria\n- An \"Electronic Health Information (EHI) Export\" section with a link to a PDF\n- A \"EchoVantage FHIR API\" section (g(10), not b(10))\n- An \"ONC Real World Testing\" section (link to separate page)\n\n### Step 3: Identify EHI export documentation\n```bash\ngrep -oiE 'href=\"[^\"]*\"' /tmp/echo-page.html | grep -iE 'ehi|export|data.dictionary|b.10|bulk'\n```\nFound one link directly relevant to EHI export:\n- `https://ensorahealth.com/wp-content/uploads/2025/08/Electronic-Health-Information-Export_EchoVantage.pdf`\n\nThe \"File Formats\" link text points to this PDF.\n\n### Step 4: Download the PDF\n```bash\ncurl -sL \"https://ensorahealth.com/wp-content/uploads/2025/08/Electronic-Health-Information-Export_EchoVantage.pdf\" \\\n  -H 'User-Agent: Mozilla/5.0' \\\n  -o downloads/Electronic-Health-Information-Export_EchoVantage.pdf\n```\nVerified: `file` confirms PDF document, version 1.6. 148,587 bytes, 1 page. Created 2025-08-15 by \"Acrobat PDFMaker 25 for Word\", author \"Catherine Baker\".\n\n### Step 5: Examine the PDF\n```bash\npdftotext Electronic-Health-Information-Export_EchoVantage.pdf -\n```\nThe single-page PDF titled \"Electronic Health Information (EHI) Export\" describes file formats for export:\n\n**Single Patient:**\n- CCD/C-CDA documents — \"will contain data required by the USCDI v1 standards\"\n- JSON — \"a lightweight format for storing and transporting data\"\n- PDF documents\n\n**Patient Population:**\n- .bak file — \"a full MSSQL Server database backup of all data for an agency that can be restored\"\n\nNo embedded URLs or attachments in the PDF (`pdfdetach -list` shows 0 embedded files).\n\n### Step 6: Check adjacent links\nThe Echo API page (`https://ensorahealth.com/onc/echo/echo-api/`) contains only FHIR API Terms of Use — legal terms for the g(10) certified API. No EHI export documentation.\n\nThe FHIR Endpoint listing (`https://ensorahealth.com/onc/echo/fhir/r4/Endpoint`) returns a FHIR Bundle of Endpoint resources listing individual customer FHIR servers (e.g., hamilton.echoehr.com). This is g(10) infrastructure, not b(10) export documentation.\n\n### Step 7: Screenshot\nFull-page screenshot taken via browser at `downloads/onc-echo-page-screenshot.png`.\n\n## What Was Found\n\nThe EHI export documentation for EchoVantage consists of:\n\n1. **A section on the ONC certification page** describing two export modes:\n   - **Single Patient Export**: Users can export EHI for a single patient \"without developer assistance\" in CCD/C-CDA, JSON, or PDF formats.\n   - **Patient Population Export**: All data for a patient population can be exported by \"submitting a support ticket via Salesforce.\" The export format is a `.bak` file — a full MSSQL Server database backup.\n\n2. **A one-page PDF** (\"Electronic Health Information Export_EchoVantage.pdf\") that lists the same file formats with brief descriptions. This is the entirety of the technical documentation.\n\nThe page notes that export content \"might vary based on a variety of factors\" including software applications in use, software version, documentation practices, configuration decisions, and \"the health system including materials not sourced from the application.\"\n\nThere is no data dictionary, no schema documentation, no field-level definitions, no sample exports, no worked examples, and no documentation of what specific data elements are included in the CCD/C-CDA, JSON, or database backup exports.\n\n## Export Coverage Assessment\n\n### Data Domain Coverage\n\nThe export documentation describes two fundamentally different approaches:\n\n**Single Patient Export (CCD/C-CDA, JSON, PDF):**\nThe PDF explicitly states these will \"contain data required by the USCDI v1 standards.\" This is a significant red flag for b(10) completeness. USCDI v1 covers only a defined subset of clinical data (demographics, problems, medications, allergies, labs, vitals, immunizations, procedures, clinical notes, goals, assessments, and health concerns). For a behavioral health EHR like EchoVantage, this almost certainly excludes:\n\n- **Treatment plans** — structured behavioral health treatment plans with outcome linkage (a core EchoVantage feature)\n- **Assessments** — standardized and custom behavioral health assessment instruments with scored results\n- **Billing and claims data** — charges, claims, eligibility, payments, remittances\n- **State reporting data** — state-specific mandatory behavioral health reporting datasets\n- **Custom clinical forms** — forms built with EchoVantage's Forms Designer\n- **Telehealth session records**\n- **Substance use recovery-specific data** (SUR)\n- **I/DD-specific clinical data**\n- **Crisis intervention documentation**\n- **Medication Assisted Treatment (MAT) data** (methadone dosing, dispensing)\n- **Documents and attachments** beyond what fits in C-CDA\n- **Visual Health Record (VHR) timeline data** — EchoVantage's signature feature\n\nThe single-patient CCD/C-CDA export appears to be the g(10) clinical summary repackaged as the b(10) export — a classic case of the (b)(10)/(g)(10) confusion described above.\n\n**Patient Population Export (.bak database backup):**\nThe MSSQL Server `.bak` file is described as \"a full MSSQL Server database backup of all data for an agency that can be restored.\" This is actually a strong b(10) approach — a full database backup would include everything in the system: clinical data, billing, assessments, custom forms, all specialty-specific data. However:\n\n- This export requires submitting a support ticket, not self-service\n- There is absolutely no documentation of the database schema — table names, column names, data types, relationships, value sets, or any other structural information\n- A recipient would need MSSQL Server to restore the backup and would then need to reverse-engineer the entire schema to understand the data\n- There's no indication of what's included or excluded from the backup\n\n### Export Format & Standards\n\n| Export Type | Format | Standard? | Appropriate? |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| Single Patient (CCD/C-CDA) | C-CDA | HL7 C-CDA | Partially — covers USCDI but not full EHI scope |\n| Single Patient (JSON) | JSON | Unspecified | Unknown — no documentation of structure |\n| Single Patient (PDF) | PDF | N/A | Renders human-readable record but not computable |\n| Population (.bak) | MSSQL .bak | Vendor-proprietary | Complete data but requires MSSQL and schema knowledge |\n\nThe JSON format is particularly opaque — is it FHIR JSON? A proprietary JSON schema? The documentation says nothing beyond \"JSON is a lightweight format for storing and transporting data.\" This is a description of the JSON format itself, not of the vendor's JSON export schema.\n\nThe .bak file format is effective for completeness but impractical for portability. It requires Microsoft SQL Server and knowledge of the proprietary database schema to be useful.\n\n### Documentation Quality\n\nThis is among the most minimal EHI export documentation possible. The entire documentation consists of:\n- ~100 words on the ONC certification page\n- A 1-page PDF with ~80 words of substance\n\nThere are no:\n- Data dictionaries\n- Schema documentation\n- Field definitions\n- Sample exports\n- Worked examples\n- API documentation specific to the export\n- User guides for performing the export\n- Screenshots of the export interface\n- Documentation of what JSON structure is used\n- Documentation of which C-CDA sections/templates are populated\n- Documentation of the database schema for the .bak backup\n\nA developer receiving any of these export files would have no basis for understanding the data structure from this documentation alone. The C-CDA would be self-describing to some extent (as C-CDA is a standard), but the JSON export and the .bak database backup are completely undocumented.\n\n### Structure & Completeness\n\n- **Granularity**: Format names only. No table names, no field names, no data types, no descriptions, no cardinality.\n- **Value sets**: None documented.\n- **Relationships**: Not documented.\n- **Versioning**: The PDF was created 2025-08-15. No version history or changelog.\n\n### Overall Assessment\n\nEchoVantage's EHI export documentation is a compliance checkbox — the bare minimum required to have something published. The approach has two significant problems:\n\n1. **The single-patient export is actually a USCDI/g(10) clinical summary**, not a comprehensive EHI export. It explicitly limits itself to \"data required by the USCDI v1 standards,\" which for a behavioral health EHR represents a small fraction of the total patient record. Behavioral health-specific data (treatment plans, assessments, custom forms, substance use data, I/DD data, crisis documentation) is where EchoVantage stores its most unique and valuable clinical information, and none of this appears to be covered by the USCDI-scoped export.\n\n2. **The population export is a raw database dump with zero documentation.** While a full MSSQL backup is technically complete, it is practically unusable without schema documentation. This shifts the entire burden of understanding the data to the recipient — who would need to restore the backup, explore hundreds of tables, reverse-engineer relationships, and guess at coded values. This doesn't satisfy the spirit of EHI export, which should enable a recipient to actually understand and use the data.\n\nThe vendor appears to have genuinely different approaches for different scopes — structured standards-based export for the USCDI subset, and a raw database dump for \"everything else\" — but the documentation for both is so thin that neither approach is practically useful to a recipient.\n\n## Access Summary\n- Final URL (after redirects): https://ensorahealth.com/onc/echo/\n- Status: found\n- Required browser: no (all content accessible via curl)\n- Navigation complexity: one_click (PDF linked directly from the page)\n- Anti-bot issues: none (Cloudflare present but not blocking)\n\n## Obstacles & Dead Ends\n- None encountered. The page loaded cleanly, the PDF downloaded without issues, and all content was accessible without JavaScript.\n- The Echo API page was checked but contains only FHIR API Terms of Use (g(10)), not EHI export documentation.\n- The FHIR Endpoint page was checked but is a g(10) endpoint listing.\n- No additional EHI export documentation was found anywhere on the site.\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":320,"cache_read_input_tokens":80477,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":320},"output_tokens":1,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"f5803685-fbf7-449d-8df2-5e72e368bc61","uuid":"b1b3b7fa-e719-4ef9-ab59-8128c600a962"}
